# Risks

Low. First-run conductor only. The deadline is an escape hatch, not a cancellation: the underlying login session is untouched, a sign-in completed after abandonment still lands via the existing auto-resume effect, and the attempt guard makes deadline-abandoned outcomes no-ops (the same settled-wave rule the deferred plugin registration watchdog documents) so a stale settle cannot mutate newer state or clobber the busy latch a newer attempt owns.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

A cloud sign-in popup that dead-ends (raw JSON on the auth callback, #19255) or is simply abandoned never settles the provision promise: nothing rejects, the `.catch` never fires, and `first-run:cloud-login-waiting` hangs forever with no recovery action.

Three pieces:

1. `armCloudLoginWaitDeadline` (`packages/ui/src/first-run/cloud-login-wait-deadline.ts`): a 90 s deadline on the visible wait, cancelled when the flow settles first.
2. `createAttemptGuard`: generation counter so outcomes of a deadline-abandoned attempt become no-ops and only the owning attempt releases the busy latch.
3. On deadline the waiting turn is replaced with a notice that carries the sign-in choice block itself: live validation showed the original cloud-oauth turn is consumed by that point, so re-seeding by id no-ops and "try again below" would have no below.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

None.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `cloud-login-wait-deadline.ts` (~90 lines) and its 5 deterministic tests (deadline fires once, cancel-before, cancel idempotent after fire, guard staleness, guard invalidate).
2. The wiring in `startCloudProvisionFlow` (`use-first-run-conductor.ts`): arm on seeding the waiting turn, cancel on settle, guarded then/catch/finally.

```bash
bun run --cwd packages/ui test -- src/first-run/cloud-login-wait-deadline.test.ts   # 5/5
bun run --cwd packages/ui typecheck                                                 # 0 errors
```

## Detailed testing steps

Start dev, click "Sign in to Eliza Cloud", then either let the popup dead-end on the #19255 callback error or simply ignore it. At 90 s the waiting turn converts to the recovery notice with a working sign-in button. Completing sign-in within the window cancels the deadline and proceeds normally.

# Product outcome

- Creates one transactional `$0` personal account and internal organization on first Telegram or phone contact without creating an `agent_sandboxes` row, API key, container, job, or automatic credit.
- Routes Telegram, Twilio, and Blooio delivery through one deterministic rowless `personal:<userId>` Shared identity; authenticated web, desktop, and mobile retain the same logical identity while its active runtime target can change.
- Adds trusted Telegram `/connect` claim through Steward and preserves the exact user, organization, personal identity, and history.
- Safely converges separately-created phone and Telegram provisional accounts only with both verified phone control and a fresh account-bound Telegram continuation. Funded, agent-bearing, API-key, session-bearing, multi-member, mature, or otherwise referenced accounts fail closed.
- Serializes provisional convergence against Dedicated cutover, persists receipts, aliases and full history, and lets an ordinary authenticated Steward retry finish a post-database convergence recovery without the original continuation.
- Removes future automatic signup/welcome credits while preserving existing balances, ledgers, paid top-ups, referrals/promotions, and credit-gated Dedicated compute.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto `origin/develop` at `3bd3b34f6ac97a7861a5de903c72e7e089adc620`; the only conflict was the paired plugin-mcp guide, resolved by preserving upstream env-declared server support and this branch's corrected resource-engine/retry contract.
- [x] `bun run verify` passes post-sync on Bun 1.3.14 and Node 24.15.0.

# Risks

**Large.** This changes Google OAuth secret handling, agent authorization, connector migration, runtime tool materialization, and the execution protocol for personal Gmail/Calendar/Drive/Docs/Sheets/Slides/People/Chat/Search. The main mitigations are vault-only durable secrets, per-call binding/scope rechecks, curated exact tool allowlists, fail-closed unsupported operations, a sole-agent-only migration backfill, and explicit multi-agent rebinding.

Google Workspace MCP remains Developer Preview. Gmail is intentionally draft-only because the official server has no send tool. Calendar is intentionally read-only because current MCP event schemas do not provide the idempotency and atomic version preconditions required by existing LifeOps write guarantees.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds a stateless MCP v2 remote-resource engine with exact calls, pagination, SSRF validation, opaque attachment generations, and injected short-lived access-token providers.
- Replaces personal Google REST execution with Google's official per-product MCP endpoints.
- Uses one direct authorization-code OAuth flow. OAuth app secrets, refresh/access tokens, and PKCE verifiers are stored behind vault/secret references; raw refresh tokens are not serialized into MCP config.
- Separates a reusable OAuth account from per-agent bindings. Bindings own selected products, allowed capabilities, default selection, status, and independent revocation.
- Dynamically materializes only reviewed namespaced tools and removes them when a product, capability, scope, binding, or connection is removed.
- Makes Cloud call official Google resources directly from its trusted runtime and revalidates the current binding, product, capability, tool, and scopes before bearer injection. The old Google broker/stub routes are removed.
- Thins `plugin-google-workspace` to OAuth, vault resolution, the multi-resource capability host, stable typed adapters, and the separate service-account Google Chat connector.
- Removes personal Google REST clients, Calendar watch/webhook infrastructure, Meet REST artifacts, Gmail Watch registry data, legacy Mockoon/REST fixtures, and old Gmail compatibility scenario contracts.
- Migrates Gmail/Calendar scenarios to deterministic MCP fixtures and exact call ledgers. Unsupported spam/bulk/send/calendar-write behavior is asserted to fail closed.
- Regenerates Cloud routes/SDK wrappers and the first-party plugin registry.

This PR completes the personal Google Workspace MCP-only cutover. It does not implement the broader Linear/Notion/GitHub SaaS deletion wave from the long-term portfolio plan.

## What kind of change is this?

Features, improvements, security hardening, database migration, and intentional removal of obsolete connector compatibility surfaces.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was updated for the direct OAuth/vault/binding model, official MCP endpoints, draft-only Gmail, read-only Calendar, removed broker/watch/mock surfaces, and the service-account Chat exception.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `packages/shared/src/contracts/google-workspace-mcp.ts` — canonical endpoint/tool/scope policy.

6. `packages/ui/src/components/connectors` and `packages/ui/src/cloud/instances/components/eliza-agent-connectors-section.tsx` — product selection and agent binding UX.

18:T81b,Closes #19352.

## What changed

- makes first contact and Steward sign-in resolve one persistent personal Eliza identity across phone, web, desktop, and native clients
- adds rowless account-scoped Shared chat with durable transcript/idempotency, metered search, credit-gated voice, and deterministic capability walls
- removes automatic signup credits and every implicit paid-compute/provisioning path in this product journey
- adds explicit Dedicated quote/confirm/cancel, singleton provisioning, authoritative sealing, and atomic Shared-to-Dedicated cutover
- replaces the generic agent dashboard with a compact personal-Eliza cockpit while preserving balance and account controls
- restores and polishes the phone-first landing page, including one-viewport responsive geometry from 320x568 through desktop

## Why

The existing product mixed generic hosted-agent provisioning with the personal-assistant entrypoint. That created paid rows before consent, fragmented identity across phone and app, and made Shared appear capable of actions it could not safely perform. This change gives the product one identity authority, one free Shared service boundary, and one explicit paid upgrade boundary.

# Risks

Medium. This intentionally turns incomplete nominally-green device runs red, hardens recorder finalization, and routes the retained focused Android CI scripts through a bundle-owning wrapper. It can expose previously hidden device-lane failures, but it does not change product runtime behavior or deploy infrastructure.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Makes every Android/iOS runner exception observable in `summary.json`, `junit.xml`, `logs/runner.log`, and the final `bundle:` line before the process exits non-zero.
- Makes bundles self-contained by copying external workflow recordings/logs into the bundle and rejects a nominal pass unless installed build ID, exact commit, inline JPG, finalized MP4, and non-empty logs are present.
- Shares strict ISO-BMFF MP4 finalization validation across Android, iOS, and inline publishing so truncated recordings are never advertised as renderable evidence.
- Waits for iOS recorder close with bounded signal escalation and emits H.264 MP4 directly.
- Stops inferring an absent installed commit from a fresh source manifest; equal build IDs with conflicting commits are rejected.
- Adds `android-playwright-e2e.mjs` so the focused onboarding/native-view/gesture/soak/launcher scripts retained by device CI also own and finalize a bundle on success or failure.
- Adds a contract test that fails if either retained device-CI shell script invokes a focused Android lane that bypasses a bundle owner.
- Documents the workflow consolidation and the separate CI rewiring gap in #19640.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and test-infrastructure hardening.

# Documentation changes needed?

The Android device-test README is updated with focused-runner usage and the current CI wiring state.

# Risks

Medium. This changes production backup schema and the managed-agent delete transaction. The material risks are retaining the wrong generation, deleting without recoverable state, crossing a tenant boundary, leaking offloaded payloads, or wedging cleanup behind one poison row.

The implementation reuses the canonical encrypted backup table and object namespace. Capture and no-snapshot waiver state are fenced to the exact bridge/lifecycle generation; retained rows are revalidated under the lifecycle lock and at commit; allocation release returns the post-trigger revision used by the final delete CAS; lookup remains organization-scoped; and cleanup isolates each candidate while deleting external bytes before metadata.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Retains the exact final full `pre-delete` backup for 30 days after a successful managed-agent deletion instead of losing it to `ON DELETE CASCADE`. It adds explicit organization, deleted-agent, deletion-attempt, and expiry metadata; leaves ordinary backups attached so they still cascade; keeps retained rows visible to restorability verification; and cleans expired inline and R2/S3 payloads in bounded provisioning-worker batches.

The reviewed repair also:

- writes deletion intent before persisting the fresh capture, so the retained-backup timestamp predicate accepts it;
- propagates every lifecycle-trigger revision through backup persistence, allocation release, and the final row-delete CAS;
- binds the supported legacy-image no-snapshot waiver to deletion attempt, environment revision, sandbox ID, and the current bridge URL;
- makes migration constraints safe when the provisioning worker's schema ensure runs before migration;
- prevents a failed R2 candidate from starving later expired rows and accounts for terminal missing-key rows;
- adds real PGlite coverage for stale revision refusal, post-trigger deletion, timestamp boundaries, and ensure-before-migrate replay.

The cleanup/migration hardening originated in [synapz-org/eliza#1](https://github.com/synapz-org/eliza/pull/1) by `hermesagent270-commits`; it was statically audited, incorporated, and refined for the repository error policy and log redaction.

## Why this approach?

The repository already owns one backup row model, KMS path, R2 namespace, verifier, and cleanup boundary. Extending those contracts is cleaner and safer than introducing a parallel pre-deletion table or object store.

# Documentation changes

`packages/cloud/shared/docs/agent-backup.md` documents retention, tenant ownership, the typed unsupported-generation waiver, fail-closed behavior, and cleanup order.

# Outcome

Validated tool-call arguments still reach the selected handler byte-exact, but every diagnostic copy now passes through one composed, non-mutating projection before it reaches a model prompt, observer, context event, summary, or persistent trajectory.

The original contribution established the right primitive and covered several executor boundaries. Exact-head review found additional leaks in the next planner/evaluator model request, compaction, generic settlement reasoning, model-call persistence, summaries, rescue prompts, and repeat-correlation material. Those gaps are repaired on the contributor branch and the complete series is rebased onto current `develop`.

# Design

`packages/core/src/security/tool-diagnostics.ts` is the canonical boundary projector:

- `composeToolDiagnosticRedactor` applies runtime-known-secret redaction first, then the shared credential-shape pass for CLI credential flags, URI userinfo, authorization headers, PEM blocks, and provider-token formats.
- `projectToolDiagnosticValue` and `projectToolDiagnosticArgs` are non-mutating, cycle/depth bounded, fully mask credential-named values, preserve non-string primitives, and structurally share unchanged plain objects.
- `projectModelCallDiagnosticValue` applies the same policy to a complete model-call record without corrupting JSON Schema semantics. Schema property/definition identifiers such as `token`, `apiKey`, and `secret` remain exact so recorded requests stay replayable; descriptions, defaults, examples, tool metadata, provider options, messages, results, and actual arguments remain scrubbed.
- Operational correlation identity (`stepId`, call/run/room/message/trace ids, action identity, `llmCallId`) remains exact. Provider-attribution byte spans are dropped through the existing canonical fallback whenever projected prompt/message bytes change, so hashes and order survive without false offsets.
- Raw execution is deliberately unchanged: `trajectory.plannedQueue` retains the exact validated call and the handler receives the exact raw parameters. Redaction occurs only when a copy leaves that ephemeral execution path.

## Covered egress boundaries

| Surface | Boundary |
| --- | --- |
| Planner queue/context/event copies | projected at copy/emit in `planner-loop.ts` |
| Pending `onToolCallEnqueued` and streaming tool-call/tool-result observers | projected before callback/emit |
| Next planner and evaluator model history | projected structurally in `trajectoryStepsToMessages` |
| Compaction summaries and terminal messages | projected before rendering |
| Repeated-failure and sub-planner correlation | raw parameters replaced with stable SHA-256 digests |
| Action-owned summaries | projected inputs plus scrubbed summary output |
| Failure rescue/synthesis prompts | projected successful excerpts and scrubbed failed causes |
| Evaluator stream/context/stored output | projected before each egress |
| `ACTION_COMPLETED` and generic action settlement | complete payload projected, including `reasoning` and future text fields |
| Core SQL trajectory LLM/action funnels | final-write projection in `TrajectoriesService` |
| Agent SQL trajectory bridge | final-write projection in `trajectory-storage.ts` |
| JSON trajectory recorder | final-write projection for tool/model/evaluation stages |
| Provider attribution | spans invalidated whenever canonical persisted input changes |

The deterministic canary test proves all of the following in one real planner/recorder path:

1. the handler receives the original synthetic credential-shaped values;
2. the second model request excludes those values from assistant tool calls and tool-result messages;
3. forced compaction excludes them;
4. streaming callbacks, lifecycle events, planner context, summaries, settlements, and the on-disk trajectory exclude them; and
5. ids, numeric telemetry, booleans, token counts, and executable JSON Schema identifiers retain their semantics.

# Risk

This changes diagnostic representations only. Handler inputs and tool execution remain exact. Consumers may now see masks or hash digests where they previously received credential material; that is the intended security contract. The schema-aware model projector and explicit identity preservation avoid the two main over-redaction risks: corrupting replay schemas and breaking trajectory joins.

No UI, provider-selection, deployment, or production configuration path changes.

# Risks

Medium. This adds a post-settlement `ACTION_COMPLETED` observer and durable engagement writes. Attribution is deliberately restricted to exact stable identities, successful applied receipts, recent rendered items, owner occurrence completion, and direct calendar time changes. Failures remain visible through `runtime.reportError` without rewriting the already-settled owner action.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Persists only surfaced morning-brief items as immutable engagement events.
- Attributes owner occurrence completions and directly applied calendar reschedules to recently rendered items by stable source identity.
- Closes expired impression windows deterministically as ignored, while preserving positive engagement.
- Adds owner-only `BRIEF recalibrate` and `BRIEF reset_recalibration` operations with durable/no-op effect receipts.
- Makes reset reversible: later evidence can relearn and demote a restored class again.
- Leaves the optimizer and PR #18584's summary-loader slice unchanged.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature — non-breaking LifeOps morning-brief engagement and owner control.

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is required. The public action schema, descriptions, receipts, and tests document the new operations.

# Risks

Medium. This is a public PSTN-to-realtime-voice boundary. It is restricted to the exact configured Eliza line, verifies Twilio's signed webhook, uses a short-lived single-use stream token, binds immutable org/user/agent/conversation/account/call claims, and retains the existing quota, revocation, interruption, and maximum-session controls.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Replaces turn-based `<Gather>` voice handling for the public Eliza number with bidirectional Twilio Media Streams backed by the existing Cartesia realtime voice session.
- Authenticates the media upgrade with a 120-second HMAC token and atomic one-use `jti` claim. The socket rejects a Twilio `start` event unless its account and call SIDs match the signed claims.
- Resolves only the exact configured public Eliza line. The tenant configuration route and duplicate outbound REST route from #19005 were removed; canonical outbound ownership remains `VOICE_CALL` / `plugin-phone`.
- Wires cross-worker revocation polling and teardown revocation into telephony sessions.
- Keeps the production feature flag, phone number, agent ID, voice ID, and endpoint out of this code PR. Production enablement remains a separately reviewable operational posture.
- Uses the current `api.eliza.app` baseline after rebasing onto `develop`.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature plus security hardening.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public API is added for tenants. This is an operational public-line transport and does not change the user-facing configuration contract.

# Testing

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto current `origin/develop`; zero conflicts.
- [ ] Full repository verify is documented as a remaining gate below.

# Risks

Medium-high because this route can dispatch paid asynchronous jobs. Each provider attempt now has a provider-specific durable credit reservation. Only a typed, verified terminal provider verdict releases that reservation and permits fallback. Known pending jobs retain their job ID and reservation for reconciliation. A submission whose acceptance cannot be determined and has no recoverable job ID does not fall back or refund; it returns `202 submission_unknown` and settles conservatively. This is intentionally platform-safe but may charge a request whose provider-side outcome cannot be recovered.

Video attempts force the synchronous durable-reservation lane even in Workers. This adds an authoritative ledger call, but avoids a non-atomic Durable Object lease-to-database-reservation handoff for jobs that may outlive the request.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds an ordered default video chain (`fal-ai/veo3`, then Atlas-hosted `vidu/q3-turbo/text-to-video`) for requests that omit `model`. Explicit model requests remain pinned to their provider.

The maintainer repair makes fallback and settlement attempt-scoped:

- Fal and Atlas distinguish terminal rejection, known pending work, and unknown submission state.
- A fallback gets a new reservation with the fallback provider/model/billing source; the prior reservation must settle to zero first.
- Atlas poll transport errors, non-2xx responses, invalid payloads, and timeout preserve the prediction ID for the existing reconciliation sweep.
- Fal pre-enqueue transport ambiguity and Atlas submit ambiguity never dispatch a second paid provider.
- Billing, affiliate/recovery identity, generation history, defaults, and pending metadata all use the provider/model that actually ran.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and billing-safety hardening. The request remains backward compatible: omitting `model` previously selected Fal and now selects the first configured default candidate. A new `202 submission_unknown` error state is returned when upstream acceptance cannot be determined safely.

# Documentation changes needed?

The Worker environment reference now identifies `ATLASCLOUD_API_KEY` as the Atlas image and fallback-video credential. This route has no standalone public API reference in the repository; the new response state is covered by route contract tests and the response carries a stable billing request ID.

- Provenance status: `self-reported`

Before publication this change went through two adversarial self-review
rounds. The second round refuted the first implementation with a finding worth
disclosing verbatim: the capture gate originally keyed on `status === "running"`
only, which made the whole mechanism **dead code on the primary v1 delete
path** — that route stamps `deletion_pending` at enqueue time, so the job
worker's later `deleteAgent()` call never sees `running`. The shipped gate
covers both surfaces, with the job-path scenario pinned as a regression test.
The same round also caught the transient-capture failure burning the delete
job's finite attempt budget where `shutdown()` retries the identical signal
for free; the `retryable` plumbing below is that repair. The audit round
additionally surfaced (and this head fixes) a self-inflicted deadlock class:
the locked retained-capture re-validation originally read through the read
pool from inside the write transaction — it now reads through the transaction
handle, which is also the correct consistency domain for a check that gates
the locked deletion intent.

- [x] Rebased onto the latest `origin/develop`; zero conflicts.

# Risks

**Medium-low, contained to the delete lifecycle and its retention artifacts.**
The diff is the sandbox service's delete path, the retention table (one
additive `expires_at` column with a DDL default, additive index), one new
org-scoped read-only recovery route, an additive best-effort purge sweep on
the existing agent-backups cron, and tests. Behavior changes only for deletes
of live dedicated agents: they now require a proven backup **of the exact
generation phase 0 inspected** (fail-closed, retryable when transient).
Unauthorized deletes, shared-tier, warm-pool, and already-captured retries
are explicitly unchanged or short-circuited, each with its own test.
`DeleteAgentResult.retryable` stays additive; no existing consumer reads it.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`shutdown()` has refused since #17180 to stop a running agent without a
current backup. `deleteAgent()` — a strictly more destructive operation — had
no such guard: the 2026-08-11 incident hard-deleted ~169 agents, and backups
covered only ~10. This PR gives the delete path the same fail-closed capture,
on **both** delete surfaces:

- **Synchronous compat path** (row still `running`): capture before deletion
  intent is stamped; a refusal leaves the row untouched with nothing for the
  reconciler to re-arm.
- **Primary v1 path** (row already `deletion_pending` from
  `enqueueAgentDeleteOnce`, container still live): the gate also covers
  `deletion_pending` rows with a live bridge; the capture fires in the job
  worker before any teardown, and a refusal leaves a recoverable tombstone
  for the next attempt.

Mechanics, mirroring `shutdown()` and then exceeding it per the audit:
phase 0 records the exact generation it inspected — container id, bridge,
lifecycle revision — **before** any bridge request, and
`prepareAgentDelete` re-compares that full tuple against the locked row for
fresh captures **and** supported-404 waivers, so a restart or replacement
completing while the unlocked fetch is in flight can never have a stale
result stamped with the current row's identity (the audit's reproduced
waiver-race). Error taxonomy carried over completely: no snapshot endpoint →
proceed flagged; transient (`SNAPSHOT_CAPTURE_TRANSIENT`) →
`retryable: true`, which `executeAgentDelete` converts to
`RetryableProvisionTransportError` so retries never burn the job's attempt
budget; anything else → fail-closed refusal.

**Retention (review round 1 P0 + audit round P1s):** the capture (or the
supported-404 waiver) persists into the cascade-immune, organization-scoped
`agent_sandbox_predeletion_backups` table — no foreign key, schema +
`ensureAgentSandboxSchema` parity, encrypted and offloaded under its own
object namespace. Retained-retry idempotence binds to
`deletion_attempt_id` + full placement (`sandbox_id` **and** `bridge_url`),
re-validated under the lifecycle lock through the transaction handle (not
the read pool). Lifecycle-revision equality is deliberately excluded from
the *retained* match — the `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger bumps the revision on
every row write, including this deletion's own CAS, so revision equality
would invalidate every retention row one update later and reintroduce the
dead-bridge non-convergence; the fresh-capture/waiver paths, where phase 0
and the lock are milliseconds apart and re-capture is possible, do enforce
revision equality.

**Bounded lifecycle + recovery (audit round):** every retention row carries
`expires_at` (default 30 days, tunable via `AGENT_PREDELETION_RETENTION_DAYS`);
the existing 6-hourly agent-backups cron purges expired rows **object-first**
(a failed object delete keeps the row for the next sweep, so the offloaded
payload can never leak), capped via `?retentionPurgeMax=`. Recovery has a
production consumer: `GET /api/v1/eliza/agents/[agentId]/backups/predeletion`
(org-scoped via `requireAuthOrApiKeyWithOrg`) returns the newest retention
row with `state_data` fetched from the object store and decrypted — valid
precisely when the sandbox row is already gone. Waiver rows return
`captureUnsupported: true` with null state, a visibly distinct
"no snapshot existed by construction" outcome.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking) — cloud lifecycle data-safety.

# Documentation changes needed?

None; behavior is documented at the code seams per repository comment policy.

deletion transaction, the real row delete cascades `agent_sandbox_backups`

audit's waiver-replacement race re-run through the actual service now
refuses with zero rows persisted; a persisted waiver lets the retry complete
with **zero** bridge contact; the purge removes expired rows and their
objects, keeps live rows, and keeps rows whose object delete failed. The
production lifecycle-revision trigger is installed in the harness so the
generation checks run against real revision-bump semantics.

**Unit (established harness):** sixteen tests in the
`deleteAgent fail-closed pre-deletion capture (#18517)` block, including the
new full-tuple cases: predecessor-generation waiver refused at the lock;
in-place restart (same placement, moved revision) invalidates a fresh
capture; retained capture with a moved bridge fails the locked re-validation;
phase 0 refuses to claim a retained capture whose bridge no longer matches.

fail**. Package `typecheck` exit 0 and Biome clean for both

# Risks

Medium deployment-automation risk. A bad target or source selection could update the wrong Railway service. The workflow therefore has no automatic trigger, binds each dispatch to a protected GitHub Environment, requires staging from `develop` and production from `main`, verifies exact protected project/environment/service/domain identities, checks the clean dispatch SHA immediately before the repository-root upload, follows only the returned new deployment id, and fails closed on health or canonical fallback drift. It never writes Railway variables or prints their sensitive values.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds the protected deployment authority that `gateway-webhook` was missing:

- manual, serialized staging/production dispatches with environment-scoped gates (production reviewer approval; staging branch/configuration gate);
- exact-SHA checkout and repository-root Railway upload with a byte-identical root config, using Railway v5.38’s supported no-path form and executable tarball-contract coverage;
- exact Railway project/environment/service/domain verification;
- canonical cloud API, agent router origin, and agent base-domain validation;
- runner-private provider responses projected immediately to canonical values and required nonblank sensitive names, including the BFF-forwarding trust gate, with raw values shredded;
- polling of the exact newly returned Railway deployment id to `SUCCESS` and proving it remains active before and after the public probes;
- applied root-config/Dockerfile/health metadata, live `/health`, a dedicated headerless forwarder-auth readiness contract whose 401 uniquely proves enforcement for `eliza-app`, active-deployment reassertion, and post-deploy canonical fallback-pair verification;
- service unit/integration coverage for enforced, disabled, project-mismatched, supplied-header, and non-GET readiness states; a six-case workflow contract test with exact protected-environment, canonical-mapping, and readiness-probe mutation checks; plus updated operator/service topology docs.

The required new protected variable is `RAILWAY_SERVICE_ID_GATEWAY_WEBHOOK`. Existing `RAILWAY_PROJECT_ID`, `RAILWAY_ENVIRONMENT_ID`, `ELIZA_APP_WEBHOOK_GATEWAY_URL`, and secret `RAILWAY_TOKEN` are also required in both `staging` and `production` GitHub Environments.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix / deployment hardening. It closes the gap where merged gateway source had no durable exact-SHA deployment path.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was updated in `.github/workflows/README.md`, the canonical Railway topology, and the gateway package guide. The package `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` pair remains byte-identical.

## Detailed testing steps

# Risks

Medium operational risk, fail-closed. Retirement can remove one loaded nginx file, but only the hard-coded staging path after exact file-type, ownership, mode, inspected SHA-256, directive allowlist, one-directive-per-line syntax, server-block, hostname, and effective `nginx -T` ownership checks. Active nginx lines containing `#` are rejected rather than guessed at, so quoted fragments cannot hide a second directive. The file is revalidated immediately before removal. Backup/rollback is active before mutation and remains active through nginx ownership/SAN/reload, renewal hook and timer health, public dual-SAN/SNI proof, CP router enrollment, environment writes, provisioning-worker restart, and final service liveness. Production and arbitrary paths are rejected.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The protected Headscale arm correctly stopped when it found a second, manually provisioned staging vhost at `/etc/nginx/conf.d/headscale-staging.conf`. This PR adds a two-step operator flow:

1. `inspect-legacy-vhost` validates and reports that exact file without changing the host. It emits metadata, a digest, directive-name counts, and the validated shape only; literal directive values never enter Actions logs.
2. After review, `retire-legacy-vhost-and-converge` requires the exact lowercase SHA-256 from inspection, then transactionally backs up and removes only those reviewed bytes while installing the canonical dual-SAN vhost. Any mismatch or later remote-convergence failure restores both prior files and reloads the restored nginx configuration.

The normal `converge` mode retains the existing unknown-owner fail-closed behavior. Inspection and retirement are registered only for staging; production cannot select them.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and operational hardening.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated the workflow inventory, Headscale deploy runbook, and Terraform control-plane runbook with the exact inspect/review/retire order and rollback contract.

# Root cause and repair

The original proposal added Cartesia as an explicit prerecorded STT provider, but paid-provider selection was not enough to make the path production-safe. The route also needs deployment-owned account pricing, atomic credit admission, authoritative usage reconciliation, bounded fetch/body execution, fail-closed response parsing, and deployment preflight that treats batch Cartesia independently from realtime voice.

This replacement:

- selects Cartesia only for exact `VOICE_BATCH_STT_PROVIDER=cartesia`;
- sends validated audio to Cartesia `ink-whisper` with the required API version, optional ISO-639-1 language, and word timestamps;
- reserves an estimated charge before dispatch, reconciles from positive finite provider duration, and refunds every failed, timed-out, or malformed result;
- requires a deployment-owned `CARTESIA_STT_USD_PER_CREDIT` rather than embedding an account-specific price;
- bounds request and response consumption with `CARTESIA_BATCH_STT_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 120000, maximum 300000) and returns a typed 504;
- structurally validates complete word timing data and never falls back after paid-provider dispatch;
- publishes Cartesia billing/timeout toggles through the trusted release workflow and permits the Cartesia key when either realtime voice or batch Cartesia owns it.

The release-workflow diff is intentionally limited to the 28 Cartesia additions and one Cartesia key-gate split. Unrelated realtime-canary changes from the shared integration tree are excluded.

- Provider key presence alone cannot select paid Cartesia.
- Missing key, missing price, invalid timeout/provider, malformed JSON, incomplete timing data, or absent authoritative duration fail closed.
- Provider bodies, API keys, filenames, and transcripts are not copied into errors or logs.
- Existing Whisper, Deepgram, and ElevenLabs routing remains explicit and covered.
- No deployment or external provider call was performed while preparing this draft.

# Risks

This changes the cancellation/sign-out lifecycle at the Cloud create/delete boundary, adds authoritative create identity fields to the compatible create response, and sends an exact conditional DELETE body. A malformed or rejected cleanup now deliberately retains the authenticated session instead of silently navigating away. There is no schema, migration, infrastructure, or deployment-variable change.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It makes cancellation after a fresh dedicated Cloud-agent allocation fail closed and assigns cleanup ownership at the correct boundary:

- The create route/client carries the authoritative `createdAt` and `executionTier` identity alongside the new agent id/name.
- If the join is cancelled after that fresh allocation, the client immediately sends a conditional DELETE with the exact name, creation timestamp, and tier. It does not wait for the provisioning job first.
- A synchronous `status: "deleted"` response, or an accepted asynchronous response with a non-empty durable delete `jobId`, means the server owns compensation. The server transaction has already validated identity, suspended billing, freed quota, marked deletion pending, and persisted recovery-owned work, so the foreground does not poll teardown to completion.
- A rejected request, `{ success: false }`, missing cleanup identity, or malformed success without an authoritative deleted/accepted receipt preserves both the cancellation and cleanup error in an `AggregateError`.
- `JoinPage` does not swallow that aggregate. It retains authentication, explains that the fresh agent could not be removed, and offers **Try again** plus an explicit **Sign out anyway** escape hatch. Superseding starts wait for prior cleanup ownership before selecting another agent.
- Both primary Join recovery CTAs (**Try again** and **Add funds**) retain the explicit dark foreground through hover. The real renderer measures 20.19:1 at rest and 16.09:1 at hover on desktop and the mobile layout; the coarse-touch profile remains 20.19:1.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking compatible response/request additions).

# Documentation changes needed?

No public documentation change is required. This corrects an internal join lifecycle and compatibility-client contract. The user-facing failure copy is registered in the canonical English catalog.

# Risks

Medium operational risk, limited to the protected Headscale arm path when it is dispatched after merge. A dispatch can request or expand a Let's Encrypt certificate and replace the nginx vhost. Both ACME and final vhost bytes are staged; rollback traps are installed before either loaded config path changes; any validation or reload failure restores the prior file bytes. The legacy DNS name must still resolve to the same control-plane VM for dual-name HTTP-01.

The workflow deliberately fails if any loaded config other than `/etc/nginx/conf.d/headscale.conf` owns either exact hostname. The expected live owner contract is exactly two records per exact hostname in that file (one HTTP and one HTTPS server). A conflicting loaded path is printed diagnostically, the prior intended vhost is restored, and unknown nginx files are left untouched. Prior staging logs do not independently prove the currently loaded owner path; the protected post-merge staging arm is the required live proof.

Renewal is now fail-closed. Every arm installs a root-owned `0755` certbot deploy hook, the hook validates both exact SANs and `nginx -t` before reload, and the arm requires `certbot.timer` to be enabled and active. The normal provisioning-worker deploy also derives the same fixed loopback API URL (`127.0.0.1:8081` production, `127.0.0.1:8080` staging), so it cannot undo the arm boundary through a public GitHub variable.

No workflow was dispatched and no Cloudflare, Hetzner, DNS, certificate, environment, or other live provider state was changed by this PR.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Keeps canonical `HEADSCALE_PUBLIC_URL` authoritative for Headscale `server_url`, control-plane router enrollment, and daemon configuration.
- Derives the exact legacy hostname and fixed loopback API/listen values from the canonical environment; dispatch callers cannot override those protected values.
- Prevents the provisioning-worker workflow from redirecting the daemon API or protected bearer key through `vars.HEADSCALE_API_URL`.
- Converges one no-http2 nginx vhost and one certificate covering both exact canonical and legacy hostnames.
- Stages and rolls back every nginx config mutation, retaining the currently loaded TLS vhost during ACME issuance.
- Requires `nginx -T` to report no conflicting-name warning and exactly two owners per hostname, both in the intended file.
- Installs a validated certbot renewal deploy hook and requires the renewal timer to be enabled and active.
- Verifies both public `/health` endpoints, then proves both SNI names serve the same SHA-256 leaf fingerprint and that each leaf has both exact SANs.
- Pins staging dispatches to `develop`, production dispatches to `main`, and checkout to `github.sha` with persisted credentials disabled.
- Removes stale runbook guidance for the retired API/listen dispatch overrides.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and deployment hardening (non-breaking migration-overlap repair).

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was required. `packages/cloud/services/headscale/README.md`, `packages/cloud/services/headscale/DEPLOY.md`, and the Hetzner control-plane runbook describe the overlap, fail-closed ownership/renewal behavior, and canonical dispatch contract.

# Risks

Low-to-medium. The change is confined to the operator-side LifeOps credential helper and its real-filesystem tests. Writes wait at most five seconds for a sibling writer and fail rather than interleave.

Lock reclamation is deliberately fail-closed for live owners: age alone never steals a lock from a PID that still exists. Dead owners are reclaimed; malformed partial records require the existing ten-second stale window. Each owner also has a random token, so an old writer's cleanup cannot remove a replacement writer's lock. As with any PID-based local lock, immediate PID reuse can conservatively delay a write until timeout; it cannot admit two writers.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Serializes the complete read-modify-rename transaction for each `~/.eliza/.env` or repo `.env` target.
- Re-reads only after the lock is held, preventing lost updates across worktrees/processes.
- Collapses every definition of a written key so last-wins dotenv parsing cannot resurrect a stale duplicate.
- Preserves unrelated lines and trailing blank lines.
- Uses randomized mode-600 temp files, removes uncommitted temp files on failure, and repairs the final mode to 600.
- Binds locks to a live PID plus random owner token; cleanup removes only the exact record acquired.
- Preserves both the primary write failure and a simultaneous lock-release failure.
- Carries #19498's exact surviving-HITL-importer contract instead of silently skipping missing paths.

### Maintainer repair

The submitted implementation reclaimed any lock whose mtime exceeded ten seconds and unconditionally unlinked the lock pathname during release. A live but delayed writer could therefore be stolen; when it resumed, its cleanup could delete the new writer's lock and admit a third writer. The submitted 22 tests all passed with that bug.

The repaired implementation checks owner liveness, adds a 128-bit owner token, conditionally removes only the observed record, and covers both failure directions with real child processes.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking operator-tooling hardening).

# Documentation changes needed?

No external documentation change. The module header documents the lock ownership contract.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

33:T1ac5,## What

Adds a **Cartesia** batch lane to `/api/v1/voice/stt` using Cartesia's prerecorded `/stt` endpoint (`ink-whisper`), selected with `VOICE_BATCH_STT_PROVIDER=cartesia`.

`VOICE_BATCH_STT_PROVIDER` pins `deepgram` | `cartesia` | `whisper` | `elevenlabs`; a pinned provider whose binding is missing **fails closed (503)** rather than silently degrading. Un-pinned selection is unchanged: free Whisper stays the default wherever `WHISPER_STT_URL` is set.

## Review change: the key-presence default was removed

The lane originally made Cartesia the un-pinned default whenever `CARTESIA_API_KEY` was present, by analogy with TTS. That was reverted on review (`4267c45`), for two reasons:

1. **It contradicts the rule stated three lines above the new branch.** The route already says: *"Deepgram prerecorded is opt-in because the same key also powers realtime Flux sessions. Key presence alone must not silently move every paid batch transcription onto Deepgram in production without an explicit rollout."* `CARTESIA_API_KEY` is in exactly that position — it powers the realtime Ink sessions (#17869 / #17997) — and it is *already set* on `eliza-cloud-api-prod` and `-staging`, so the default would have taken effect on deploy with no rollout step.
2. **The TTS analogy does not carry.** TTS has no free lane; batch STT does. `WHISPER_STT_URL` is set on the live worker, so the flip would have converted working zero-credit transcription into 402s for every such user — a silent free→paid change whose only mitigation was an operator noticing and setting the variable back.

Everything else in the lane is unchanged: `reserve → dispatch → bill/refund`, the `elevenlabs/scribe_v1` pricing proxy, fail-closed **502 + refund** on any upstream/parse failure with no cross-provider fallback, `X-API-Key` auth, transcript/filename log redaction, seconds→ms word-span mapping, and BCP-47 → ISO-639-1 language normalization.

Batch REST (`POST https://api.cartesia.ai/stt`, multipart) is used rather than a one-shot wrapper around the realtime Ink WS — the WS only accepts raw 16 kHz mono PCM in 100 ms frames, which a Worker cannot transcode container formats to. Batch REST accepts exactly the container formats the route's magic-number allowlist already admits.

The realtime path is untouched: this PR only modifies the batch route, its types, and its tests.

## Tests

`bun test packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/stt/route.test.ts` → **65 pass / 1 skip (live gate) / 0 fail**, covering the full override matrix, fail-closed-on-missing-binding for every pin, the wire contract (headers/model/multipart/language-normalization/exact bytes), reserve→bill→usage, 402-before-call, word-span mapping, and malformed/5xx/transport → 502 + refund + no-secret-logs. The key-presence case is now asserted in the opposite direction: a configured Cartesia key alone must leave batch STT on free Whisper, with no reservation and no billing. Targeted Biome clean.

## Deploy notes

- No behavior change on deploy. Cartesia batch STT activates only when an operator sets `VOICE_BATCH_STT_PROVIDER=cartesia`; worth one canary STT request when they do.
- `Cartesia-Version: 2026-03-01` (the documented version for `/stt`).
- Cartesia batch returns word timestamps only — responses have `words`, never `segments` (downstream must tolerate absence, as it already must for the ElevenLabs lane).
- `CARTESIA_API_KEY` is already set on `eliza-cloud-api-prod` + `-staging`; no new secret needed.

# Risks

Medium, tightly bounded to passkey sign-in and first-passkey enrollment. The SDK patch retains its existing backward-compatible default for other callers; this UI caller opts out of implicit enrollment so it can require an explicit user action. Hard server, network, MFA, and device-verification failures remain visibly distinct and never start enrollment.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Canonical Steward deliberately returns privacy-preserving discoverable-credential options with an empty `allowCredentials` list rather than revealing whether an email has a passkey. Browsers therefore report the same `NotAllowedError` for “no matching credential” and for cancellation. The old UI treated every non-UV error as enrollment and sent an unsolicited setup OTP.

This change:

- adds an SDK option that lets a caller disable implicit 404 registration while preserving the current default for existing callers;
- corrects SimpleWebAuthn v13 invocation to the required `{ optionsJSON }` shape for authentication, registration, and MFA;
- exercises the real Steward SDK and SimpleWebAuthn stack through deterministic `navigator.credentials.create` registration and `navigator.credentials.get` MFA boundaries;
- treats browser-owned no-credential/cancellation as an ambiguous recovery state;
- limits cancellation and user-verification recovery to browser/WebAuthn failures, so a 5xx response containing `user verification` remains a hard server error;
- offers explicit **Use Magic Link** and **Set up passkey** actions, and sends no email until the user chooses one;
- clears stale recovery actions when a same-mounted-component retry receives a hard server error;
- preserves the Passkey control’s accessible `accent-foreground` color on hover;
- registers the new `@stwd/sdk` patch in the canonical patch checksum manifest.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and authentication safety/UX improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

No separate product documentation change is required. The SDK patch declaration, component comments, focused real-SDK tests, checksum manifest, and browser proof describe the compatibility and privacy boundary.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with `steward-login-section.tsx`, then the real-SDK fallback test, component capability matrix, SDK patch, and `passkey-explicit-recovery.spec.ts`.

## Detailed testing steps

1. Run the focused UI and real-SDK tests; confirm 21/21 pass, including real registration and MFA SDK flows through deterministic browser credential boundaries.
2. Run the Chromium spec at desktop and mobile sizes with a virtual authenticator and canonical 200 discoverable options containing `allowCredentials: []`.
3. Confirm the initial ceremony sends zero Magic Link and zero OTP requests and exposes explicit recovery actions.
4. Hover **Passkey** and confirm the computed foreground/background resolve to `--accent-foreground` / `--accent-hover` with contrast at least 4.5:1.
5. Without remounting, retry after the recovery state against a hard 500 containing `User verification service unavailable`; confirm both recovery actions clear and no email is sent.
6. Repeat, choose **Set up passkey**, and confirm exactly one OTP request. Choose **Use Magic Link** separately and confirm exactly one Magic Link request.
7. Run `scripts/security/verify-patches.sh`, `bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app`, and root `bun run verify`.

# Risks

Low. This is a read-only owner/admin endpoint with `Cache-Control: no-store`; it returns an explicit redacted DTO rather than persistence records. The new public query policy accepts scoring horizons from 1 through 365 days. The response intentionally includes the full active ledger, including later and undated commitments, so active-ledger growth can increase response size.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Exposes `GET /api/lifeops/commitments/regret-audit` as the first production consumer of the existing regret-audit builder.
- Enforces the existing private owner/admin route gate and scopes reads to the runtime's current agent, regardless of any `agentId` query parameter.
- Reads only `open` and `tracked` commitments and returns a deliberately narrow response without `agentId`, `sourceKey`, or arbitrary metadata.
- Defaults `horizonDays` to 7 and validates a bounded 1–365 range before any ledger read.
- Preserves builder semantics: the horizon affects urgency scores and reasons; it does not hide later or undated active obligations.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature (non-breaking change which adds functionality).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is required for this bounded backend surface. The route contract is documented by its DTO, comments, and integration tests; the larger product integration remains tracked by #14864.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/commitment-regret-audit-route.test.ts`. It exercises the route, service, repository, and real PGlite database together. Then inspect the registered-route auth assertions in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/routes/plugin.test.ts`.

## Detailed testing steps

Using Node 24.15.0 and Bun 1.3.14:

```bash
bun install --frozen-lockfile
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-personal-assistant test -- test/commitment-regret-audit-route.test.ts src/routes/plugin.test.ts test/commitment-ledger.test.ts
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-personal-assistant typecheck
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-personal-assistant lint:check
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-personal-assistant verify
bun run verify
git diff --check
```

Expected focused result: 3 files and 29 tests pass. The real-PGlite route tests verify redaction, tenant/status filtering, no mutation, stable tied-row ordering, and typed rejection of malformed persisted source, kind, confidence, and timestamp values.

Two things, both found by talking to the live agent in Discord.

**1. `plugin-notes` had no chat action at all.** It ships views + a `NotesService`, but its CRUD resolves only through `PAGE_DELEGATE` against an **open view**. A chat-only deployment (Discord, Telegram) therefore could not reach notes. Live, "make a note" fell through to whatever else matched — `DATABASE` hand-writing SQL, or the room-gated owner todo surface — and the note was silently lost.

**2. A notes search could not find notes.** Asked **"search my notes for the wifi"**, the live planner received:

```
contexts: ["documents"]
candidateActionNames: ["SEARCH_DOCUMENTS"]
```

`NOTES` appeared **zero times** in the entire trajectory, and the user was told *"no notes found matching wifi"* — for a note that exists.

## Root cause of (2)

The action advertised `list` / `read` / `show` and never **search** or **find**. No `SEARCH_NOTES` simile, no "search" in the routing hint. So retrieval had no reason to surface it, and a documents search won by default — a reasonable read of an intent the action never claimed.

The fix keys on **shape** — *any read scoped to the user's notes* — instead of adding three more phrasings, because enumerating just moves the bug to the next wording a user invents. It also names the wrong destinations explicitly: documents, files and database do not index notes, so they answer "nothing found" rather than "wrong tool".

## Design notes

- `NOTES` wraps the **same** `NotesService` the view uses — one record, one store. No second persistence path.
- `SAVED_NOTES` renders that same snapshot into turn context, so recall needs no memory write. Deliberately **not** mirroring note text into a memory record: that copy would survive an edit or a delete.

<!-- attribution-row:status -->
- Attribution status: self-reported39:T1061,## Problem

`Character.templates` can override the framework's canned failure replies, but no shipped
preset sets any of them. So when an agent hits a rate limit, runs out of credits, or has no
model provider configured, it drops its persona and emits voice-neutral framework text. The
character visibly breaks at exactly the moment the user is already frustrated.

## What this does

Adds in-character failure replies to the default `eliza` preset, and wires `templates` through
the preset contract so any persona can carry them. Each reply says what happened **and** what
the user can do about it:

| key | reply |
| --- | --- |
| `rateLimitedReply` | my model provider is throttling me right now. give it a few seconds and send that again, it should go through. |
| `insufficientCreditsReply` | my model provider is out of credits, so i can't answer this one. waiting won't fix it. add credits or raise the quota and i'll be back, then send that again. |
| `noModelProviderReply` | i don't have a model provider set up yet, so i can't answer anything. set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, or OPENROUTER_API_KEY in your environment, or sign in to eliza cloud (ELIZAOS_CLOUD_API_KEY), then try me again. |
| `authFailedReply` | my model provider isn't accepting my key right now, so i can't work through this. worth checking that the key is still valid and the account is active, then send that again. |
| `transientFailureReply` | something broke on my end and i didn't get an answer out. it wasn't anything you did. try that again in a moment. |

Written in the shipped Eliza voice: lowercase-friendly, brief, sincere, no emoji, no em-dashes.

Only the `eliza` preset carries templates. The field is optional, so the other eight personas
keep the voice-neutral framework defaults until someone writes theirs. The runtime emits these
verbatim (no handlebars pass, no per-locale resolution), exactly like the English framework
strings they replace.

## Verification

- `packages/core` (vitest): `message.character-failure-templates.test.ts` → **17 passed / 17**.
  Covers each key rendering on its real error condition, fallback to framework default when a
  character sets no template, and no cross-leaking between keys.
- `packages/shared` + `packages/agent` (bun): preset + build-character-config suites →
  **51 pass, 0 fail** across 3 files.
- `bunx biome check` on touched source files: clean.

# Risks

Low. Isolated settings billing BrandCard copy, a11y, and wrap changes. Checkout
API contract is unchanged. Invalid amounts now fail inline instead of only via
toast, and the buy button stays enabled until checkout starts.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Applies better-* HIGH/MEDIUM to the billing credit hero, buy-credits form, and
invoice-list BrandCards:

- Accessible names, form submit on Enter, and `aria-invalid` / described-by errors
- Instructional amount errors next to the field
- Verb-first buy button that stays labeled while checkout is in flight
- Tabular numbers on money; stronger contrast on muted amount chrome
- Invoice list reflows at 320px instead of a 600px min-width scroller
- Invoice status keeps its text and adds an icon cue (not color-only)

This is not a SettingsRow conversion.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

- Confirm the buy button stays enabled on an empty amount and Enter shows

- Confirm a valid amount shows tabular confirmation and starts checkout
- Narrow to 320px and confirm invoices stack instead of horizontal-scrolling
- Confirm paid/open status show text plus an icon

3c:T1311,## What

Applies one invariant to two more sites: **a scoped, filtered or truncated tool result must say so in the text the model reads.** Otherwise the model reports it as complete and the user is told something false.

Already applied to `ls`, `TASKS manage_issues` and `PLUGIN list` — this is the same shape, found by auditing for it rather than waiting for the next incident.

**plugin-relationships entity list** — the read is `entityStore.list({ type, limit })`. A caller-supplied `kind` and the row cap both narrow it, and neither appeared in the result, so a partial list read as the whole relationship graph.

**plugin-inbox read** — entries come from `getByClassification(...)` whenever a classification is supplied. An empty classification-scoped read rendered as a bare empty inbox, which reads as *"you have nothing"* rather than *"nothing in this category"*.

Both now name the filter and the pre-filter count **where it is measured**, and say how to widen. No count is printed that was not read from data already in hand — an invented total is worse than none.

### Why this pattern keeps recurring

Five live incidents traced to the same missing rule, including a reply that said *"You're not connected to anything right now"* while Discord was actively delivering it, and `ls` answering **91** typescript files when the real recursive count was **1215**.

## Exact candidate

- Base: `90c2cd8de48eef5920746c78b9aa54e82ffdb48c`
- Head: `4f7f0c81f61994d338f1608c6f57b885b4c40ef7`
- Paths: three under `plugins/plugin-relationships`, two under `plugins/plugin-inbox`

Grafted onto develop's current files and checked against a symbol-deletion guard, so nothing merged since is reverted.

# Risks

Low and homepage-local. The rendered changes are deterministic landing copy, responsive containment, and browser assertions. The claim matcher is deliberately a conservative test guard for this fixed marketing script, not an exhaustive natural-language classifier or a runtime security boundary. It is not imported by the landing component at runtime. This PR changes no backend, authentication, agent runtime, deployment, shared-package export, or provider configuration.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Replaces the primary landing demo with a typed, conversation-memory-only Rome exchange. Every scripted assistant line and card remains inside the capability available to a fresh immediate agent after the Shared-agent rollback.
- Adds a homepage-local contract that fails the exact rendered script on recognized external-action claims. Its regression matrix covers every prior legacy claim, the exact future-tense flight check-in/aisle-seat line, all eight reviewer synonym probes, and matched benign conversation-memory guards.
- Fixes 200% zoom horizontal containment for desktop controls without changing the intentional vertically scrollable reflow.
- Preserves the upstream whole-mobile tap target, full-screen conversation, animated thread/composer accessibility, and native contact sheet. Browser coverage opens the sheet at normal and 200%, traverses every contained option, and keeps the copy-number fallback observable.
- Preserves HTTPS Discord, the existing copy-number fallback, and WhatsApp fail-closed behavior through package/browser assertions.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No standalone documentation change is needed. The implementation and tests carry the homepage-local capability and responsive contracts.

into one SettingsRow that repeated the group title) into one SettingsRow per
action. Logs, filters, dialog results, and the clipboard draft sit on
dedicated stacked rows.

Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)

40:T82a,## Summary

Fixes issue where compound capabilities (e.g., `gmail.manage` requiring both `gmail.modify` AND `gmail.settings.basic`) were incorrectly recorded as fully granted even when only a subset of constituent scopes were approved. This caused re-auth to re-request previously-denied scopes, breaking the least-privilege principle and issue #18543.

## Changes

- **connector-account-provider.ts**: Enhanced `matchCapabilityFromScope` to validate ALL constituent scopes are present before recording a compound capability as granted
- Added `allAvailableScopesLower` parameter to track full scope set during grant normalization
- Updated `normalizeGrantedCapabilities` to pass complete scope set for verification
- Partial grants are now silently excluded from granted capabilities, allowing re-auth to properly detect the gap

## Test evidence

- **158/158 regression tests passing** in `connector-account-provider.compound-capability.test.ts`
  - Validates compound capabilities (gmail.manage, calendar.full) require ALL constituent scopes
  - Single-scope capabilities (gmail.read, gmail.modify) unaffected by the change
  - Partial grants correctly excluded from granted capabilities
  - Re-auth properly re-requests denied scope components
  - Mixed compound and simple capability grants handled correctly

## Problem this fixes

When a user partially granted a compound capability (approved some but not all required scopes), the system would:
1. Record the compound capability as fully granted
2. On re-auth, skip re-requesting the denied scopes (since they were "already granted")
3. Leave the account in a broken state with incomplete permissions

Now:
1. Partial grants are excluded from recorded capabilities
2. Re-auth detects the gap and properly re-requests missing scopes
3. Only fully-granted compound capabilities are recorded

## Verification

- Built successfully with `bun run build --cwd plugins/plugin-google-workspace`
- All tests pass: `bun run test --cwd plugins/plugin-google-workspace`
- No lint/typecheck regressions: `bun run verify`

# What changed

The implementation closes five fail-open numeric parsing paths under `packages/scripts`:

| Surface | Before | Exact-head behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Test-console port | `0` logged `:0` while binding an unrelated ephemeral port; `1e4` bound 10000 | canonical TCP port `1..65535`; invalid startup exits 2 with a bounded named error |
| `dev-all` ports | `abc`, `1e4`, and `080` reached URLs/probes as different values | all five port envs share the canonical port parser and fail before plan/prepare output |
| `trajectory list --limit` | partial values changed the row count; bare/missing values silently used 20 | positive canonical decimal only; empty, terminal-bare, and next-flag forms fail before listing |
| `run-all-tests --concurrency` / `TEST_CONCURRENCY` | partial values changed worker count; explicit empty degraded to serial; malformed values leaked a stack | canonical `1..32`; missing/empty CLI values and malformed flag/env values use the controlled exit-2 usage boundary; empty env remains unset |
| Message corpus counts/seeds | hex/scientific/leading-zero values were coerced | complete safe decimal integer grammar, retaining signed seeds and each field's existing bounds |

The shared `lib/cli-numbers.mjs` parser rejects hex, exponent, fraction, leading-zero, surrounding-whitespace, trailing-garbage, unsafe, and out-of-range forms. Valid `--concurrency=3` remains covered because `.github/workflows/develop-pr.yml` invokes that exact form.

# Review repairs

- Distinguished an absent concurrency flag from `--concurrency=` and from a missing separate value.
- Routed malformed CLI and environment concurrency through `failUsage()` (exit 2, no stack).
- Rejected `trajectory list --limit`, `--limit --agent ...`, and `--limit=` instead of folding them into omission.
- Moved test-console port parsing to the direct startup boundary so route imports remain side-effect free.
- Repaired the owning test-console entrypoint test, which the submitted focused lane missed: it now allocates a real valid loopback port instead of relying on forbidden production port `0`.
- Added bounded startup errors for malformed `dev-all` and test-console port configuration.
- Retained and expanded the contributor's concurrent spawned-CLI regression suite, with co-authorship recorded in the repair commit.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Closes three fail-closed gaps left after the first #19060 implementation merged:

1. A present `data` property is authoritative and must be a plain object; invalid nested data cannot fall through to root `content` as a flattened message.
2. Only an actually absent timestamp defaults to receipt time. Present `null`, `undefined`, blank, whitespace, boolean, fractional, unsafe, negative, or non-finite values drop the message.
3. JSON parsing errors remain HTTP 400, while runtime delivery failures propagate through `Bot`, return HTTP 500, report through the runtime diagnostic boundary, and remain retryable.

Bug fix.

# Risks

Low. The diff touches one operator smoke script and its new test file. Behavior changes to weigh (revised per review — counts and context sizes now carry per-consumer operational maxima rather than `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`):

- All numeric env knobs now resolve once at module load through `resolveMobileSmokeNumericEnv()`; a malformed value fails the script immediately — including invocations that would only print usage — instead of running a device lane with a silently wrong timeout. For a smoke lane that bills device/CI minutes, failing before any work is the intended direction.
- Unset/empty values keep the exact documented defaults (pinned by tests); valid explicit values are unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`packages/app/scripts/mobile-local-chat-smoke.mjs` parsed every timing/count env knob with bare `Number.parseInt`, so malformed values silently became different valid numbers. Confirmed live reproductions (mock `/api/health` + hub-ready + hanging `/messages/stream`):

- `ANDROID_FULL_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS=1e3` → "timed out after 1ms" in 0.12s (`parseInt("1e3")===1`); control `800` behaved correctly. `8abc` → 8ms. `2147483648` → `TimeoutOverflowWarning`, Node clamps the timer to 1ms while the message still claims 2147483648ms.
- `ANDROID_HEALTH_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS=abc` → NaN fails the `timeoutMs > 0` gate, so the AbortController is never armed and the probe fetch hung until an external SIGKILL — a typo disabled the timeout entirely.
- The same unguarded parse covered `ANDROID_TRANSIENT_RETRY_*`, `ANDROID_STABILITY_*`, `ANDROID_LOCAL_INFERENCE_READY_*`, `ANDROID_SMOKE_MODEL_CONTEXT_SIZE`, and `IOS_FULL_BUN_SMOKE_CONTEXT_SIZE`.

The fix reuses the script directory's existing fail-closed helpers (`parsePositiveSafeInteger` / `parseNonNegativeSafeInteger` / `MAX_TIMER_DELAY_MS` from `lib/host-agent.mjs`): one exported resolver returns all knobs, timer knobs are bounded `1..2147483647`, delays allow `0`, counts require `≥1`, unset/empty keep the documented defaults, and any other token throws naming the variable before any device or API work.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking for valid input; malformed input now fails loudly before spending device/CI time).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The file header documents the fail-closed contract.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`resolveMobileSmokeNumericEnv()` in `packages/app/scripts/mobile-local-chat-smoke.mjs`, then `mobile-local-chat-smoke.test.mjs` (co-located, matching the directory's existing `*.test.mjs` convention).

## Detailed testing steps

1. `bun test packages/app/scripts/mobile-local-chat-smoke.test.mjs` — 27 pass / 0 fail at this head: every documented default pinned exactly (all eleven knobs plus the null size override), each coercion rejected (`1e3`, `8abc`, `2147483648`, `abc`, fractions, hex, and canonical-spelling rejections like `0008`), and per-knob operational bounds enforced: context sizes ≤ 32768 (the 131072 format ceiling is the documented phone OOM width and is an explicit rejection case), counts ≤ 10000, size override ≤ 1 TiB, plus relational and composite work-inclusive budgets (stability samples ≤ attempts; each loop's attempts × per-attempt work-plus-delay ≤ 24h, with the stability budget nesting the full transient-retry budget and readiness fetches carrying the bounded probe timeout).

47:Td53,# Relates to

Promotes the already-merged develop fixes #19393 and #19432 to `main` so the production phone line (808-788-1821) stops playing the Twilio `<Say>` robot greeting.

# Background

## What problem does this solve?

Production `main` still serves the original #19321 TwiML: `<Say>Hi, you're connected to Eliza.</Say>` before `<Connect><Stream>`. TwiML `<Say>` with no `voice` attribute is Twilio's default robotic TTS, so every caller hears a canned robot voice before the real Cartesia agent voice takes over. Phone QA on the prod line reproduces it on every call.

Develop already fixed this twice over:

- #19393 (merged 2026-08-14) removed the `<Say>` opener entirely and replaced it with a live Cartesia-voiced session greeting ("hello? who's this?" / "hey whats up") spoken in the agent's own voice, plus interruptibility fixes.
- #19432 (merged 2026-08-14) removed the cold first-turn stall the `<Say>` was masking, by prewarming scope/character/admission during the inbound webhook (cold TTFA ~6.5s down to ~4.2s, within ~0.5-1.0s of warm).

Neither promotion reached `main`, so prod still runs the robot greeting.

## What does this PR do?

Cherry-picks the two develop commits onto current `origin/main`, conflict-free:

- `3e88b13c13d` fix(cloud): make phone voice responsive and interruptible (#19393)
- `d9ed54afbe8` fix(cloud): remove cold first-turn voice stall (#19432)

`buildRealtimeVoiceTwiML` no longer takes a `greeting` and emits only `<Connect><Stream>`. The Cartesia session greeting in `media/route.ts` covers the first moments of the call in the brand voice. `buildTerminalVoiceTwiML` (error paths like NOT_CONFIGURED) intentionally keeps `<Say>`; robot voice is acceptable for terminal error prompts.

# Risks

Low. This tightens the public JSON upload boundary: omitted `contentType` still
defaults to `text/plain`, while a supplied empty string no longer takes that
default and now receives HTTP 400, as do parameter-only, malformed, and
non-string values. Callers that were relying on malformed or explicitly empty
MIME values being silently accepted will need to omit the field or send a valid
MIME type.

Original-file storage now intentionally records the normalized lowercase MIME
essence, so its stored media content type no longer retains caller casing or
parameters. The original valid caller value remains available as
`metadata.fileType`; single and bulk route tests pin this split.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Validates an optional upload MIME once for single and bulk document requests.
- Uses the validated lowercase MIME essence consistently for byte decoding,
  original-file storage, document-service routing, and canonical metadata.
  Stored media MIME therefore drops caller casing and parameters by design.
- Retains the caller's valid original MIME string only as `metadata.fileType`;
  focused single and bulk assertions pin both representations.
- Preserves legacy text-extension UTF-8 byte decoding and lowercase `.mdx` routing to `text/markdown` for single and bulk uploads.
- Preserves legacy binary MIME decoding; `application/x-sh` remains base64-backed.
- Adds a real `DocumentService` regression over the checked-in OpenZeppelin PDF
  for canonical, uppercase, and parameterized `application/pdf` values.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking for valid upload requests).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is needed. The accepted API contract is
unchanged for omitted and valid MIME values; the malformed-input behavior is
covered by focused tests.

# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Makes `formatSlackDate` safely reject invalid and unrepresentable dates.
- Anchors both Slack permalink parsers and rejects unrelated hosts, extra path segments, malformed widths, invalid digits, and trailing garbage.
- Normalizes canonical 16-digit and seconds-only 10-digit permalink timestamps.
- Supports Slack's documented 15-digit examples by left-padding the five-digit fractional field. The official threaded example exposes a six-decimal `thread_ts`, confirming that the omitted digit is a leading fractional zero: https://docs.slack.dev/reference/methods/chat.getPermalink/
- Documents the deliberate bare-link contract and the mrkdwn extraction path.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and public parser hardening.

4c:T1210,## Problem

A cloud agent is created with a **name and nothing else** — no persona is collected at
creation time — so `agent_sandboxes.agent_config` was written as `{}`. Every downstream
reader then fell through to a stub:

- the shared turn and the dedicated first-boot window synthesised `You are <name>, a helpful assistant.`
- a dedicated container (which receives the same blob as `ELIZA_AGENT_CHARACTER_JSON`) landed on
  the runtime's bare `defaultCharacterSystemTemplate`, because a user-chosen agent name matches
  no bundled preset

Net effect: **the desktop app gives you full Eliza, and the cloud gives you a stub.** The 9-preset
catalog in `packages/shared/src/character-presets.characters.ts` is never referenced anywhere in
`packages/cloud`.

## Fix

Seed the shipped default preset (`CHARACTER_DEFINITIONS[0]`, "eliza") into `agent_config` at
create time. That fixes all four readers at once — `projectSharedAgentCharacter`,
`ElizaSandboxService.buildRuntimeBootstrapAgent`, `buildWarmClaimCharacterPayload`, and the
container's `applySandboxCharacterFromEnv` — and leaves the row carrying a real, editable
persona for the character UI. No new character was authored; this reuses what already ships.

Shape decisions, dictated by those four readers and documented in the module header:

- keys sit at the **top level** of `agent_config` (only the shared turn tolerates a nested
  `{ character: {...} }`; the other three are flat-only)
- `name` is deliberately **not** seeded — every reader prefers `agent_config.name` over the
  `agent_name` column while `updateAgentProfile` renames only the column, so a seeded name
  would win forever and strand the agent under its creation-time name
- `{{name}}` tokens are preserved rather than expanded, matching how the presets ship so a
  rename keeps propagating
- `messageExamples` use the strict `{ examples: [...] }` group form, because the warm-claim
  push validates against the strict CharacterSchema and silently drops the legacy form

## Verification

- `bun test --isolate src/lib/services/default-agent-character.test.ts` → **9 pass / 0 fail**
- `bun test src/lib/services/eliza-sandbox.test.ts` → **207 pass / 0 fail** (204 on develop; +3 added)
- `bun test --isolate src/lib/services/shared-runtime/` → **208 pass / 0 fail** (13 files)
- warm-claim + create-idempotency + dedicated-bootstrap + tier-upgrade + shared-billing → **53 pass / 0 fail**
- cloud API create-path suites (7 files) → **64 pass / 0 fail**
- `bunx biome check` on all 5 touched files → clean; `typecheck` → 0 errors

Owning-package full sweep: 6745 pass / 15 fail. **All 15 failures are pre-existing on
`origin/develop`** — each was re-run with these 5 files stashed and failed identically
(onboarding-host-contract ×7, pairing-token ×5, steward-url ×2, web-push config ×1).
